### Exam-style questions (mixed topics)

**Q1269.** Human insulin, from gene to bacteria
<!-- id: vce-bio34-x1 -->
*Multi-part question · 11 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Biology exam, Section B*

Insulin is a protein hormone made by beta cells in the pancreas. The insulin gene is transcribed and translated, and the protein is then processed and exported from the cell.

**a.** Describe two differences between the structure of DNA and the structure of mRNA. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Part of the template strand of a gene reads 3′ TAC GGA TTC 5′. Write the mRNA sequence transcribed from it, and the amino acids it codes for. Use these codons: AUG = Met, CCU = Pro, AAG = Lys. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Insulin is exported from the cell. Describe the path the insulin protein takes from the ribosome to the outside of the cell. *(2 marks)*

To make human insulin in bacteria, a DNA copy of the insulin gene is made from insulin mRNA (complementary DNA, or cDNA). It is inserted into a plasmid that carries a gene for resistance to the antibiotic ampicillin.

**d.** Explain why the cDNA copy is used instead of the insulin gene taken directly from human DNA. *(1 mark)*

**e.** Explain why the plasmid and the cDNA are cut with the same restriction enzyme, and name the enzyme that joins them together. *(2 marks)*

**f.** After transformation, the bacteria are grown on agar containing ampicillin. Explain why only some bacteria form colonies, and why growing on ampicillin does not prove that a colony contains the insulin gene. *(2 marks)*

**Q1270.** A new virus: immunity, vaccines and resistance
<!-- id: vce-bio34-x2 -->
*Multi-part question · 11 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Biology exam, Section B*

A newly identified respiratory virus has a protein, called S, on its outer surface. People infected for the first time take about a week to recover.

**a.** Explain how the S protein causes B cells that recognise it to multiply. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Describe two ways antibodies against S help to remove the virus. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Explain why cytotoxic T cells are needed to clear an infection that is already established. *(2 marks)*

A vaccine containing harmless copies of the S protein is developed.

**d.** Explain how this vaccine gives long-term protection against the virus. *(2 marks)*

An antiviral drug blocks one of the virus's enzymes. A few years after the drug comes into wide use, a strain of the virus that is resistant to the drug has become common.

**e.** Explain how the resistant strain became common. *(3 marks)*

**Q1271.** Photosynthesis, Rubisco and a gene-edited crop
<!-- id: vce-bio34-x3 -->
*Multi-part question · 11 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Biology exam, Section B*

Rubisco is the enzyme that fixes carbon dioxide in the light-independent stage of photosynthesis. It can also bind oxygen instead, a process called photorespiration that wastes energy and reduces the plant's output of glucose.

**a.** Name where the light-independent stage occurs, and the two products of the light-dependent stage that it uses. *(2 marks)*

**b.** On hot, dry days the rate of photosynthesis in C3 plants falls. Explain why. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Explain how C4 plants reduce photorespiration. *(2 marks)*

Scientists use CRISPR-Cas9 to edit a gene in a crop plant so that it tolerates drought better.

**d.** Describe the roles of the guide RNA and the Cas9 enzyme. *(2 marks)*

**e.** Explain how cutting the DNA can change the gene. *(1 mark)*

**f.** Give one benefit and one concern of growing gene-edited drought-tolerant crops. *(2 marks)*

**Q1272.** Birds on two islands
<!-- id: vce-bio34-x4 -->
*Multi-part question · 9 marks · Original question in the style of the VCE Biology exam, Section B*

About two million years ago, a population of seed-eating birds colonised two islands separated by deep water. The birds rarely fly between them. Today the birds on the two islands are separate species.

**a.** Explain how the two species could have formed. *(3 marks)*

**b.** A storm kills most of the birds on one island, at random. Name this event and explain its effect on the gene pool. *(2 marks)*

The DNA sequence of one gene was compared in three bird species. The number of differences is shown below.

| Species pair | A and B | A and C | B and C |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Number of differences | 4 | 10 | 9 |

**c.i.** Which two species are most closely related? *(1 mark)*

**c.ii.** Explain why the number of DNA differences can be used to estimate how long ago two species diverged. *(1 mark)*

**d.** A fossil bird is found in a rock layer between two layers of volcanic ash dated at 2.4 and 2.1 million years old. Estimate the fossil's age and name the dating methods used. *(2 marks)*

**Q1273.** Challenge: insulin from bacteria
<!-- id: vce-bio34-cx1 -->
*Challenge question*
*Multi-part question · 11 marks · Original challenge question in the style of the VCE Biology exam, Section B*

Researchers want bacteria to produce human insulin. They extract mRNA for insulin from human pancreatic beta cells and make complementary DNA (cDNA) from it.

**a.** Explain why cDNA made from mRNA is used rather than the insulin gene cut from a human chromosome. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Name the enzymes used to insert the cDNA into a plasmid, and describe the role of each. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Before cloning, the cDNA is amplified by PCR from 50 copies through 20 cycles. How many copies are produced, and what is the role of the primers? *(2 marks)*

The plasmid has an ampicillin-resistance gene and a lacZ gene containing the insertion site. Bacteria are treated to take up plasmids, then grown on agar containing ampicillin and X-gal.

**d.** Explain how the researchers can identify colonies of bacteria that contain the recombinant plasmid. *(3 marks)*

**e.** In a beta cell, insulin passes through the rough endoplasmic reticulum and the Golgi apparatus. Describe the role of each, and explain why bacteria may need extra steps to make active insulin. *(2 marks)*

**Q1274.** Challenge: responding to a new virus
<!-- id: vce-bio34-cx2 -->
*Challenge question*
*Multi-part question · 12 marks · Original challenge question in the style of the VCE Biology exam, Section B*

A new respiratory virus spreads through a town. Most people infected for the first time are ill for about a week; people infected a second time months later have few symptoms.

**a.** Describe one physical and one chemical barrier in the respiratory tract that can stop the virus entering cells. *(2 marks)*

**b.** Explain why cytotoxic T cells, rather than antibodies, are needed to clear virus from infected cells. *(2 marks)*

**c.** Explain why a second infection months later causes few symptoms. *(2 marks)*

**d.** After a year, variants appear that antibodies from earlier infections bind poorly. Explain how these variants become common. *(3 marks)*

**e.** R₀ for the virus is 4. Using 1 − 1/R₀, find the herd immunity threshold. *(1 mark)*

**f.** Monoclonal antibodies are given to high-risk patients. Give one advantage and one limitation compared with vaccination. *(2 marks)*
